We introduce a composite objective that integrates masked epsilon-MSE reconstruction, thresholded identity divergence in FR embedding space, directional feature alignment, and source-similarity suppression to balance adversarial attack and visual realism. Optionally, LLaVA-generated attribute prompts enhance fine-grained semantic details without reintroducing identity cues. Under the black-box evaluation protocol, Adv-TGD attains an average attack success rate (ASR) of 85.90% across IR152, IRSE50, MobileFace, and FaceNet, surpassing the semantic SOTA baseline Adv-CPG by +6.25 points, diffusion-based makeup method DiffAIM by +3 points, and noise-based P3-Mask by +16 points. Despite its strong attack efficacy, Adv-TGD preserves high visual fidelity (PSNR = 27.15 dB, SSIM = 0.981). Furthermore, we demonstrate the flexibility of our framework by successfully extending it to in-the-wild datasets (LADN), general object classification (ImageNet), and transformer-based diffusion models (FLUX.1).

</details>


### [31] [Precision-Aware Illumination-Disentangled Vision Transformer for Spacecraft 6D Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11619)
*Zongwu Xie,Yifan Yang,Yonglong Zhang,Guanghu Xie,Yang Liu,Shuo Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a new model, PAID-ViT, for robust spacecraft 6D pose estimation under challenging conditions, achieving improved robustness and reduced translation error.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Monocular spacecraft 6D pose estimation is difficult due to illumination variation, specular reflection, shadowing, weak texture, and background interference, which can destabilize pose regression.

Method: The proposed PAID-ViT model separates pose-relevant structure tokens from illumination-sensitive appearance tokens, estimates patch reliability, and uses foreground mask supervision to preserve silhouette cues.

Result: Experiments on SPEED+ V2 suggest that PAID-ViT reduces translation error and improves robustness in the challenging sunlamp domain.

Conclusion: The proposed PAID-ViT model offers a robust solution for spacecraft 6D pose estimation under challenging conditions, with ablation studies supporting the effectiveness of its components.

Abstract: Vision sensors provide a lightweight solution for spacecraft proximity operations, but monocular spacecraft 6D pose estimation remains difficult under illumination variation, specular reflection, shadowing, weak texture, and background interference. These factors make local visual evidence spatially unreliable and can destabilize pose regression. This article proposes a Precision-Aware Illumination-Disentangled Vision Transformer (PAID-ViT) for robust spacecraft pose estimation.The proposed model separates pose-relevant structure tokens from illumination-sensitive appearance tokens, estimates patch reliability before pose aggregation, and uses foreground mask supervision to preserve silhouette cues. A parameter-free geometric recovery module converts normalized crop coordinates, log-depth, and a continuous 6D rotation representation into camera-frame rotation and translation. Experiments on SPEED+ V2, the SPEED+ validation/lightbox/sunlamp evaluation configuration used in this study, suggest that PAID-ViT reduces translation error and improves robustness in the challenging sunlamp domain, while ablation studies support the complementary roles of illumination disentanglement, reliability-aware token aggregation, mask supervision, and training-side regularization.

</details>


### [32] [Adapting Vision-Language Models from Iconic to Inclusive for Multi-Label Recognition Without Labels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11626)
*Cheng Chen,Jingyu Zhou,Yifan Zhao,Jia Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposed an unsupervised framework to adapt vision-language models for multi-label image recognition without labeled data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision-language models struggle with multi-label images due to their tendency to focus on the most iconic object, conflicting with the nature of multi-label learning.

Method: The framework consists of two stages: 'cutting' with a multi-sampling response estimator to prevent focus on a single object, and 'sewing' with multi-object blend adaptation to adjust labels for a multi-label distribution.

Result: The framework significantly outperforms existing unsupervised approaches on four public datasets and even surpasses some weakly supervised baselines.

Conclusion: The proposed framework demonstrates the potential of adapting pre-trained vision-language models for comprehensive visual understanding without manual annotations.

Abstract: Understanding multi-label images remains a challenging task in computer vision. With the rapid progress of vision-language multimodal learning, vision-language models (VLMs) enable zero-shot recognition without labeled data. However, due to their intrinsic design, these models often prioritize the most iconic object and omit other contextual positives. This intrinsic bias conflicts with the nature of multi-label learning, thereby limiting their applicability. In this work, we propose an unsupervised framework that adapts VLMs from iconic recognition toward inclusive understanding, enabling label-free multi-label image recognition. Our approach consists of two key stages, ``cutting'' and ``sewing'': In the cutting stage, we present the multi-sampling response estimator to prevent the model from concentrating only on one single object. In the second sewing stage, the multi-object blend adaptation is introduced to adjust the labels to better conform to the multi-label distribution while preserving the intrinsic characteristics of the original model within only one epoch. Extensive experiments show that our framework significantly outperforms existing unsupervised approaches on four public datasets, even surpassing several representative weakly supervised baselines. These results demonstrate the potential of adapting pre-trained VLMs for more comprehensive visual understanding without manual annotations. Our code is publicly available at https://github.com/iCVTEAM/TailorCLIP.

</details>


### [33] [Motion Reinforces Appearance: RGB-Skeleton Gated Residual Fusion for Micro-Gesture Online Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11645)
*Jialin Liu,Xinwen He,Pengyu Liu,Jiale Shi,Huaijuan Zang,Yanbin Hao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes DyFADet+, a dual-stream RGB-skeleton framework for micro-gesture online recognition, achieving an F1 score of 40.88 on the SMG dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Micro-gesture analysis is crucial for inferring spontaneous emotions, but it is challenging due to the subtlety of body movements and the need for multi-modal cues.

Method: The proposed DyFADet+ model uses a dual-stream RGB-skeleton framework, projecting both modalities into shared multi-scale temporal embeddings and fusing them through a gated residual module.

Result: The method achieves an F1 score of 40.88 on the SMG dataset, ranking 2nd in the Micro-gesture Online Recognition track.

Conclusion: The proposed DyFADet+ model effectively improves micro-gesture online recognition by leveraging both RGB and skeleton modalities.

Abstract: Micro-gesture analysis attracts increasing attention for inferring spontaneous emotion from subtle body movements. Micro-gesture online recognition, which localizes and classifies each gesture instance in untrimmed videos, is a core task in the 4th EI-MiGA-IJCAI Challenge. Compared with typical temporal action detection, MGR emphasizes the localization and classification of actions, requiring the model to output the start time, end time, and category of each micro-gesture. Moreover, since micro-gestures are highly spontaneous, relying solely on a single modality makes it difficult to capture the complete and accurate multi-modal cues. In this work, we propose DyFADet+, which extends DyFADet into a dual-stream RGB-skeleton framework. In our model, both modalities are projected into shared multi-scale temporal embeddings and fused through a gated residual module, which adaptively injects skeleton motion into the RGB representation rather than using naive concatenation. Finally, these fused features are decoded by a Dynamic TAD head for online classification and boundary regression. On the SMG dataset, our method achieves an F1 score of 40.88, ranking 2nd in the Micro-gesture Online Recognition track.

</details>


### [34] [Learning Instance-Adaptive Low-Rank Orthogonal Subspaces for Clothes-Changing Person Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11661)
*Dong-Woo Kim,Tae-Kyun Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Ortho-ReID is proposed for clothes-changing person re-identification, which uses a transformer-based Basis Maker to extract clothing-invariant representations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for clothes-changing person re-identification rely on adversarial learning to disentangle clothing features, but this work aims to improve performance by explicitly modeling a low-rank clothing subspace.

Method: Ortho-ReID uses a transformer-based Basis Maker to refine a shared, low-dimensional clothing prior into an instance-adaptive low-rank subspace, and extracts clothing-invariant representations via direct geometric constraints.

Result: Ortho-ReID achieves state-of-the-art performance on PRCC, Celeb-reID-light, and LaST, with competitive results on LTCC.

Conclusion: The proposed Ortho-ReID method improves clothes-changing person re-identification performance by explicitly modeling a low-rank clothing subspace and extracting clothing-invariant representations.

Abstract: Clothes-changing person re-identification (CC-ReID) aims to recognize individuals despite drastic appearance changes caused by clothing variation. While existing methods rely on adversarial learning to disentangle clothing features, we propose Ortho-ReID, which explicitly models a low-rank clothing subspace from VLM text descriptions and extracts clothing-invariant representations via direct geometric constraints. A critical component is our transformer-based Basis Maker, which refines a shared, low-dimensional clothing prior into an instance-adaptive low-rank subspace through cross-attention with image patches, enabling robust clothing feature extraction even under varying visibility conditions. This instance-adaptive subspace is supervised via alignment with clothing text embeddings, while identity features are extracted via a learnable projection head and geometrically constrained to be strictly orthogonal to it. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on PRCC (+5.9% top-1), Celeb-reID-light (+3.5%), and LaST (+5.3%), with competitive results on LTCC.

</details>


### [35] [ARGUS: Stacked Multi-View Identity Mosaic Injection for Subject-Preserving Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11670)
*Zijie Meng,Jiwen Liu,Yufei Liu,Chengzhuo Tong,Xiaoqiang Liu,Yuanxing Zhang,Yulong Xu,Pengfei Wan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Argus, a novel framework for subject-preserving video generation, achieves state-of-the-art results by introducing a dynamic identity memory and large-scale counterfactual self-supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video generation methods struggle with subject preservation across various conditions such as motion, viewpoint changes, and expression shifts, mainly due to the limitations of the point-reference paradigm.

Method: The Argus framework utilizes Stacked Multi-View Identity Mosaic Injection (SMII) to convert identity evidence into a compact dynamic distribution, and employs several techniques like no-cross-pair counterfactual training and Adaptive Self-Likeness Guidance to improve robustness.

Result: Argus achieves state-of-the-art results on OpenS2V-Eval Human-Domain and HardID-Celeb benchmarks, demonstrating significant improvements in subject-preserving video generation.

Conclusion: The proposed Argus framework effectively addresses the challenges of subject-preserving video generation by introducing a dynamic identity memory and large-scale counterfactual self-supervision, leading to state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks.

Abstract: Subject-preserving video generation is not solved by frontal-face similarity alone: a generated person must remain recognizable across motion, large viewpoint changes, expression shifts, occlusion, scale variation, and conflicts among text, first-frame, and identity references. We argue that the central bottleneck is the point-reference paradigm, which collapses identity into a single static observation entangled with pose, accessories, lighting, background, and camera statistics. We introduce Argus, a Wan-based framework centered on Stacked Multi-View Identity Mosaic Injection (SMII). SMII converts MLLM-selected image/video identity evidence into a 3*3 stacked mosaic, synchronizes the mosaic with the current diffusion time, and injects it as negative-time read-only memory in Wan's native token space. This turns identity from an external clean adapter or a single reference image into a compact dynamic distribution. Around SMII, an MLLM Identity Director selects informative identity moments and resolves condition conflicts, while no-cross-pair counterfactual training, Temporal Identity Annealing, and Adaptive Self-Likeness Guidance improve robustness without paired subject-video supervision. We further release HardID-Celeb, a public-figure identity-stress benchmark, and introduce YawScore and OccScore to probe large-yaw and first-frame-occlusion robustness. Argus achieves state-of-the-art results on OpenS2V-Eval Human-Domain, reaching 64.38 Total Score, 71.86 FaceSim, 51.62 NexusScore, and 79.14 NaturalScore. On HardID-Celeb, Argus obtains 76.80 FaceSim and improves YawScore and OccScore by 12.60 and 15.10 points over the strongest baselines, demonstrating that dynamic identity memory and large-scale counterfactual self-supervision are highly effective for subject-preserving video generation.

</details>


### [36] [Parameter-Efficient Adapter Tuning for Tabular-Image Multimodal Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11682)
*Jiaqi Luo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposed TI-Adapter, a fine-tuning framework for efficient multimodal learning using tabular and image data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for multimodal learning are computationally expensive or limit task-specific adaptation.

Method: TI-Adapter uses adapter-based fine-tuning, freezing the pretrained tabular encoder and adapting the image branch.

Result: TI-Adapter achieves competitive or better predictive performance than full fine-tuning with fewer trainable parameters.

Conclusion: TI-Adapter enables efficient multimodal adaptation with balanced performance and practical efficiency.

Abstract: Tabular-image multimodal learning aims to improve predictive modeling by jointly using structured tabular attributes and visual data. Although pretrained encoders provide strong modality-specific representations, full fine-tuning can be computationally expensive, while keeping encoders frozen may limit task-specific adaptation. We propose the Tabular-Image Adapter (TI-Adapter), a modality-specific adapter-based fine-tuning framework for efficient multimodal adaptation. TI-Adapter freezes the pretrained tabular encoder and learns an adapter after the extracted tabular embedding, while adapting the image branch with embedding-level and bottleneck-level adapters instead of full fine-tuning. Experiments on 20 tabular-image datasets show that TI-Adapter achieves competitive or better predictive performance than full fine-tuning while using substantially fewer trainable parameters. Ablation studies further demonstrate the importance of adapter placement for balancing performance and practical efficiency.

</details>


### [37] [Reason, Then Re-reason: Cross-view Revisiting Improves Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11683)
*Chaofan Ma,Zhenjie Mao,Yuhuan Yang,Fanqin Zeng,Yue Shi,Yingjie Zhou,Xiaofeng Cao,Jiangchao Yao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a framework called Reason, then Re-reason (ReRe) for spatial reasoning from egocentric videos, which allows for revisiting and revising conclusions based on complementary viewpoints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors argue that spatial reasoning from egocentric videos is challenging due to limited observable evidence and existing methods rely on single-turn inference, which forces models to rely on semantic priors rather than verifiable evidence.

Method: The ReRe framework consists of two phases: the Reason Phase, where a MLLM forms a spatial hypothesis from the original video, and the Re-reason Phase, where it verifies or revises the hypothesis by observing a synthesized novel-view video generated by a Geometry-to-Video pipeline.

Result: The ReRe framework substantially boosts open-source MLLMs to rival proprietary state-of-the-art performance on VSI-Bench and STI-Bench benchmarks.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that the proposed ReRe framework enables effective cross-view revisiting and revising of spatial reasoning conclusions, leading to improved performance in spatial reasoning tasks.

Abstract: Spatial reasoning from egocentric videos is inherently challenging because the observable evidence is constrained by the camera trajectory. Existing methods rely on single-turn inference, forcing models to resolve geometric ambiguity through semantic priors rather than verifiable evidence. We argue that spatial reasoning should be revisitable: conclusions formed under limited evidence should remain open to revision when complementary viewpoints become available. Building on this insight, we propose Reason, then Re-reason (ReRe), a training-free, inference-time framework with two phases: in the Reason Phase, an MLLM forms a spatial hypothesis from the original video; in the Re-reason Phase, it verifies or revises the hypothesis by observing a synthesized novel-view video. To enable effective cross-view revisiting, we design a Geometry-to-Video pipeline that renders strategically complementary novel views from predicted 3D geometry. These views feature an elevated, oblique perspective with scene-spanning coverage, while preserving the MLLM's native video interface without architectural modifications. Extensive evaluations on VSI-Bench and STI-Bench demonstrate that ReRe substantially boosts open-source MLLMs to rival proprietary state-of-the-art performance. Project page: https://zhenjiemao.github.io/ReRe/

</details>


### [38] [DroneShield-AI: A Multi-Modal Sensor Fusion Framework for Real-Time Autonomous Drone Threat Detection, Behavioral Intent Classification, and Swarm Intelligence in Contested Airspace](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11687)
*Marius Bayizere*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents DroneShield-AI, a unified framework for detecting and analyzing UAV threats, achieving high detection accuracy and low latency on commodity hardware.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The emergence of Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) threats as a significant security challenge in the 21st century.

Method: DroneShield-AI integrates six processing layers: RF signal classification, acoustic motor-signature detection, visual detection, sensor fusion, Behavioral Intent Classification Engine (BICE), and Graph Neural Network Swarm Intelligence Module (GNN-SIM).

Result: The framework achieves 96.1% detection accuracy, 3.2% false alarm rate, AUC-ROC: 0.981, and 142ms end-to-end latency on commodity CPU-class hardware.

Conclusion: The proposed DroneShield-AI framework provides an effective and efficient solution for detecting and analyzing UAV threats, with a high degree of accuracy and low latency, at an affordable system cost.

Abstract: Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) threats have emerged as a defining security challenge of the 21st century. This paper presents DroneShield-AI, a unified open framework integrating six processing layers: RF signal classification, acoustic motor-signature detection, YOLOv8-based visual detection, evidence-weighted sensor fusion, a Behavioral Intent Classification Engine (BICE), and a Graph Neural Network Swarm Intelligence Module (GNN-SIM). BICE introduces the first systematic six-class threat taxonomy for drone flight patterns, enabling predictive operator alerts with a 30-second advance-warning horizon. GNN-SIM is the first open framework for adversarial multi-drone formation analysis using Graph Attention Networks. Evaluated on three publicly available real-world datasets, the fused pipeline achieves 96.1% detection accuracy, 3.2% false alarm rate, AUC-ROC: 0.981, and 142ms end-to-end latency on commodity CPU-class hardware at approximately $500-$780 USD total system cost. All code, model weights, and simulation datasets are publicly released at submission.

</details>


### [39] [RankVR: Low-Rank Structure Perception and Value Recalibration for Robust Composed Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11689)
*Jiale Huang,Zixu Li,Zhiheng Fu,Zhiwei Chen,Qinlei Huang,Yupeng Hu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes RankVR, a framework for Composed Image Retrieval (CIR) that addresses challenges in noisy datasets by introducing Global Structure Consistency Perception (GSCP) and Adaptive Semantic Value Calibration (ASVC) modules.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The prevalence of Noisy Triplet Correspondence (NTC) in large-scale datasets constrains model performance in CIR, and existing denoising methods have limitations.

Method: The paper proposes RankVR, which includes GSCP to decouple clean samples from structural noise and ASVC to distinguish high-value hard clean samples.

Result: Extensive experiments on FashionIQ and CIRR benchmark datasets show that RankVR significantly outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: RankVR demonstrates superior robustness in noisy environments and effectively addresses challenges in CIR.

Abstract: Composed Image Retrieval (CIR) constitutes a pivotal paradigm requiring models to perform joint reasoning on reference images and modification texts. However, the prevalence of Noisy Triplet Correspondence (NTC) in large-scale datasets severely constrains model performance. Existing denoising methods either target binary mismatches or rely on scalar-based point-wise estimation, neglecting rich global structural correlations among sample populations and dynamic value variations during training, thereby yielding suboptimal results. This paper identifies two critical unresolved challenges: Global Structural Inconsistency of Semantic Correlations and Hard Sample Discrimination Uncertainty. To address these, we propose RankVR, a framework designed to construct a robust CIR model via global structure consistency and dynamic value perception. Specifically, we introduce the Global Structure Consistency Perception (GSCP) module, which utilizes the Effective Rank of the Correlation Matrix to decouple clean samples from structural noise. By measuring rank difference, GSCP identifies samples disrupting macroscopic semantic symmetry. Furthermore, we develop the Adaptive Semantic Value Calibration (ASVC) module to distinguish high-value hard clean samples. By integrating training potential and reliability, it dynamically quantifies the semantic value of each triplet, ensuring effective utilization of hard samples while suppressing noise characterized by logical conflicts. Extensive experiments on the FashionIQ and CIRR benchmark datasets demonstrate that RankVR significantly outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods, validating its superior robustness in noisy environments.

</details>


### [40] [MedCTA: A Benchmark for Clinical Tool Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11702)
*Tajamul Ashraf,Hyewon Jeong,Fida Mohammad Thoker,Bernard Ghanem*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces MedCTA, a benchmark for evaluating medical AI agents on multimodal clinical tasks, and finds that even state-of-the-art models are brittle in multi-step clinical tool use.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current benchmarks for medical AI agents focus on simple recognition or single-turn question answering, but do not evaluate more complex tasks like planning, tool recruitment, and rollout reliability.

Method: The authors introduce MedCTA, a benchmark comprising 107 real-world clinical tasks with clinician-verified executable trajectories over 5 deployed tools, and evaluate 18 open- and closed-source multimodal models.

Result: The results show that even frontier systems are brittle in multi-step clinical tool use, with autonomous rollouts dominated by protocol failures, premature stopping, and incorrect tool recruitment.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that strong backbone perception does not translate into reliable agentic behavior in clinical settings, and that MedCTA provides a rigorous testbed for auditing, diagnosing, and advancing trustworthy medical AI agents.

Abstract: To make clinically grounded decisions, medical AI agents are expected to go beyond simple recognition and be capable of tool retrieval, evidence acquisition, and integration. Existing benchmarks largely evaluate isolated perception or single-turn question answering, and therefore provide limited visibility into failures of planning, tool recruitment, and rollout reliability. We introduce MedCTA, a benchmark for evaluating medical tool agents on clinician-validated, step-implicit tasks grounded in realistic multimodal clinical inputs, including radiology images, pathology slides, and reports. MedCTA comprises 107 real-world clinical tasks with clinician-verified executable trajectories over 5 deployed tools, and supports process-aware evaluation of tool selection, argument validity, execution stability, trajectory fidelity, and outcome quality. We benchmark 18 open- and closed-source multimodal models and find that even frontier systems remain brittle in multi-step clinical tool use: autonomous rollouts are dominated by protocol failures, premature stopping, and incorrect tool recruitment, while gold-standard tool routing yields large but still incomplete gains. These results show that strong backbone perception does not translate into reliable agentic behavior in clinical settings. MedCTA provides a rigorous testbed for auditing, diagnosing, and advancing trustworthy medical AI agents. The dataset and evaluation suite are available at https://ivul-kaust.github.io/MedCTA/

</details>


### [41] [ERN-Net : Evolving Reason Node-Net for Document Binarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11710)
*Hsin-Jui Pan,Sheng-Wei Chan,Jen-Shiung Chiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ERN-Net, an evolving reason node-net, efficiently binarizes document images by enhancing degradation-sensitive regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Document image binarization is challenging due to degradation-sensitive regions like faint strokes and noisy backgrounds.

Method: ERN-Net uses evolving reason nodes and multi-scale reasoning, with ConvNeXt-Tiny as the backbone, and DIBCO-based pretraining.

Result: ERN-Net shows effectiveness under low-data and low-memory settings on DIBCO-style benchmarks.

Conclusion: ERN-Net provides an efficient solution for document image binarization, balancing accuracy and memory usage.

Abstract: This paper presents ERN-Net, an Evolving Reason Node-Net for efficient document image binarization. ERN-Net enhances degradation-sensitive regions, such as faint strokes, broken characters, and noisy backgrounds, through evolving reason nodes and multi-scale reasoning. We further compare ResNet-101, ConvNeXt-Tiny, and ConvNeXt-Base, and find that ConvNeXt-Tiny provides the best practical trade-off between accuracy and memory usage. In addition, DIBCO-based pretraining improves binarization performance without increasing model memory consumption, requiring only about 1.5 additional training hours. Experiments on DIBCO-style benchmarks show that ERN-Net is effective under low-data and low-memory settings.

</details>


### [42] [Ouroboros-Spatial: Closing the Data-Model Loop for Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11719)
*Enhan Zhao,Wei Wu,Yuanrui Zhang,Xueliang Zhao,Di He*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The authors propose Ouroboros-Spatial, a self-evolving training framework that improves spatial reasoning in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) by dynamically generating and filtering training samples based on the model's evolving capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing approaches to spatial reasoning in MLLMs rely on large-scale, statically curated datasets, which can be data-inefficient and lead to the model being trained on samples that are either trivial or overly difficult.

Method: The proposed framework, Ouroboros-Spatial, uses a frozen proposer to generate spatial question-answer (QA) pairs and executable code for deriving reliable ground truth, while a learnable solver is fine-tuned on accepted samples and provides a per-sample prediction confidence signal to guide the proposer in the next iteration.

Result: Ouroboros-Spatial substantially improves Qwen3-VL-4B and Qwen3-VL-8B models across six spatial reasoning benchmarks, using an order of magnitude fewer training examples than recent large-scale curated datasets, with absolute gains of 9.9 and 6.8 points on VSI-Bench for the 4B and 8B models, respectively.

Conclusion: The Ouroboros-Spatial framework offers a more efficient and effective approach to improving spatial reasoning in MLLMs by dynamically adapting the training distribution to the model's evolving capabilities.

Abstract: Spatial reasoning remains a persistent challenge for multimodal large language models (MLLMs). Existing approaches largely rely on large-scale, statically curated datasets, where all training samples are treated uniformly regardless of the model's evolving capabilities. This static paradigm is inherently data-inefficient: training capacity is often spent on samples that are either trivial or overly difficult for the model at its current stage. To address this limitation, we propose Ouroboros-Spatial, a self-evolving training framework in which the model plays dual roles as a proposer and a solver. In each iteration, a frozen proposer generates spatial question-answer (QA) pairs from 3D scene metadata and raw video frames, together with executable code for deriving reliable ground truth. A learnable solver is then fine-tuned on the accepted samples, and its per-sample prediction confidence is used as a difficulty signal. This signal is fed back to the proposer in the next iteration, guiding it to generate questions better matched to the solver's current capabilities. Through this closed-loop design, the training distribution co-evolves with model ability, reducing redundant trivial examples while filtering out ambiguous or uninformative samples with limited learning value. Across six spatial reasoning benchmarks, Ouroboros-Spatial substantially improves Qwen3-VL-4B and Qwen3-VL-8B while using an order of magnitude fewer training examples than recent large-scale curated datasets. On VSI-Bench, it yields absolute gains of 9.9 and 6.8 points for the 4B and 8B models, respectively, enabling both to outperform a wide range of strong open-source and proprietary baselines.

</details>


### [43] [Multi-View In-Cabin Monitoring System for Public Transport Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11739)
*Evgeny Gorelik,Kenny Dean Karrow,Fikret Sivrikaya,Sahin Albayrak,Christian Baumann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A multi-view in-cabin monitoring dataset for public transportation is introduced, featuring synchronized RGB and depth images, LiDAR data, and annotations for 3D human pose estimates and occupant bounding boxes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a dataset and tools for developing and evaluating multi-view in-cabin perception models for digitalized and partly automated public transportation, specifically for a German city bus.

Method: The dataset includes 9,136 synchronized samples with annotations, a calibration pipeline, and pseudo-labeling for 3D human pose estimates and oriented 3D bounding boxes. A nuScenes-format conversion and benchmarking of representative multi-view 3D detection models are also provided.

Result: The dataset and tools are made available for comparative evaluation and small-scale training of multi-view in-cabin perception models, supporting the development of more accurate and robust models.

Conclusion: The introduced dataset and tools aim to facilitate research and development in multi-view in-cabin perception for public transportation, enabling the creation of more efficient and safe transportation systems.

Abstract: We introduce a multi-view in-cabin monitoring dataset for public transportation with synchronized RGB and depth images from four inward-facing cameras and a rotating LiDAR covering the vehicle interior of a digitalized and partly automated German city bus. The dataset contains 9.136 synchronized samples with annotations and is accompanied by a calibration and pseudo-labeling pipeline that generates 3D human pose estimates and oriented 3D bounding boxes for occupants. We further provide a nuScenes-format conversion and benchmark representative multi-view 3D detection models (e.g., Lift-Splat-Shoot and BEVFusion), supporting comparative evaluation and small-scale training of multi-view in-cabin perception models. The dataset and tools are available at https://github.com/EvgenyGorelik/multiview_incabin_dataset.

</details>


### [44] [UniReason-Med: A Shared Grounded Reasoning Interface for 2D-to-3D Transfer in Medical VQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11740)
*Mengzhuo Chen,Yan Shu,Chi Liu,Hongming Piao,Xidong Wang,Derek Li,Bryan Dai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces UniReason-Med, a framework that uses grounded reasoning supervision from 2D medical images to improve 3D medical visual question answering (VQA) by aligning both input types through a common reasoning interface.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to leverage abundant 2D medical images to enhance 3D medical VQA, as 3D medical images are often scarce and expensive to annotate.

Method: The method involves a single-checkpoint framework, UniReason-Med, which processes either 2D images or slice-serialized 3D volumes, generating interleaved textual reasoning and localized visual evidence through shared box syntax, region-token injection, and a common grounded reasoning policy.

Result: The results show that joint 2D+3D grounded supervision substantially improves 3D reasoning over 3D-only training, while grounding and region-token injection consistently benefit both 2D and 3D tasks.

Conclusion: The study concludes that a shared grounded reasoning interface can transfer reasoning structure from 2D images to slice-serialized volumetric medical understanding, improving 3D medical VQA performance.

Abstract: We study whether grounded reasoning supervision from abundant 2D medical images can improve 3D medical VQA when both input types are aligned through a common reasoning interface. We introduce UniReason-Med, a single-checkpoint framework that processes either a 2D image or a slice-serialized 3D volume at inference time, generating interleaved textual reasoning and localized visual evidence through shared box syntax, region-token injection, and a common grounded reasoning policy. To train this interface, we construct UniMed-CoT, a 220K instruction-tuning dataset with interleaved textual reasoning and grounded visual evidence, including 170K 2D and 50K 3D samples. Through supervised fine-tuning followed by outcome-level reinforcement learning, UniReason-Med learns to generate grounded reasoning traces without IoU/Dice-based localization rewards during RL. Data-mixture and component ablations show that joint 2D+3D grounded supervision substantially improves 3D reasoning over 3D-only training, while grounding and region-token injection consistently benefit both 2D and 3D tasks. These results suggest that a shared grounded reasoning interface can transfer reasoning structure from 2D images to slice-serialized volumetric medical understanding. The code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/IQuestLab/unireason-med.

</details>


### [45] [From Prompts to Tokens: Internalizing Causal Supervision in Vision-Language Model for Multi-Image Causal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11745)
*Haoping Yu,Yuanxi Li,Jing Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes BridgeVLM, a model that internalizes visual causal reasoning by inducing a causal graph from multi-image inputs and executing it through RAMP layers in an LLM decoder.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Large vision-language models (VLMs) are brittle at visual causal reasoning tasks, especially for interventional and counterfactual queries over multi-image inputs.

Method: The authors propose BridgeVLM, which induces a causal graph from multi-image inputs and converts it into structured Causal Tokens executed by RAMP layers injected into the LLM decoder.

Result: BridgeVLM achieves 54.4% accuracy on intervention tasks on CausalVLBench, improves results on Causal3D from 43.6% to 49.0%, and substantially improves causal structure learning on CausalVLBench (F1: 33.4% → 75.1%).

Conclusion: The proposed BridgeVLM model improves visual causal reasoning by internalizing causal mechanisms, leading to better performance on various causal reasoning tasks.

Abstract: Visual causal reasoning is essential for understanding and intervening in the physical world, requiring identification of causal variables from visual inputs and reasoning over intervention effects. Despite recent progress, large vision--language models (VLMs) remain brittle at such tasks, especially for interventional and counterfactual queries over multi-image inputs. Most existing explorations inject causal knowledge via textual prompts, leaving causal mechanisms external to model execution and limiting reliable control during inference. To address this problem, we propose BridgeVLM, which internalizes visual causal reasoning by inducing a causal graph from multi-image inputs and converting it into structured Causal Tokens executed by RAMP layers injected into the LLM decoder for causal message passing. We further introduce a unified training interface M3S for fine-grained causal supervision from different granularities (local/global level). BridgeVLM achieves 54.4% accuracy on intervention tasks on CausalVLBench (vs. 33.2% with prompt-level supervision), improves results on Causal3D from 43.6% to 49.0%, and substantially improves causal structure learning on CausalVLBench ($F_1$: 33.4% $\rightarrow$ 75.1%).

</details>


### [46] [AnchorEdit: Maintaining Temporal Consistency in Multi-turn Image Editing via Causal Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11751)
*Hang Xu,Xiaoxiao Ma,Guohui Zhang,Yu Hu,Siming Fu,Jie Huang,Lin Song,Haoyang Huang,Nan Duan,Feng Zhao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AnchorEdit, an autoregressive diffusion-based framework, is proposed for high-resolution, long-term multi-turn image editing, addressing identity drift and error accumulation issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current models struggle with identity drift and error accumulation in multi-turn image editing due to their misalignment with the causal, sequential nature of interactive editing.

Method: AnchorEdit uses a three-stage training curriculum: identity-preserving single-turn pretraining, causal AR forcing fine-tuning with self-rollout strategy, and consistency distillation for efficient generation. A memory mechanism is introduced during inference to anchor the initial subject identity.

Result: AnchorEdit achieves state-of-the-art results, maintaining exceptional subject fidelity and instruction following even over 10+ interaction rounds on a new high-resolution multi-turn editing benchmark.

Conclusion: AnchorEdit effectively addresses the challenges of multi-turn image editing by providing a framework that ensures stability and consistency across extended editing trajectories.

Abstract: Multi-turn image editing is essential for iterative design, yet current models often struggle with identity drift and error accumulation over successive steps. While existing research leverages video priors for consistency, their reliance on bidirectional attention is fundamentally misaligned with the causal, sequential nature of interactive editing. In this paper, we propose AnchorEdit, the first autoregressive (AR) diffusion-based framework designed specifically for high-resolution, long-term multi-turn editing. AnchorEdit bridges the gap between video priors and causal inference through a three-stage training curriculum: identity-preserving sing-turn pretraining, causal AR forcing fine-tuning with a novel self-rollout strategy to mitigate exposure bias, and consistency distillation for efficient 4-step generation. During inference, we introduce a memory mechanism to anchor the initial subject identity and ensure stable extrapolation across extended editing trajectories. To evaluate performance, we provide a new high-resolution multi-turn editing benchmark designed to stress-test long-horizon stability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AnchorEdit achieves state-of-the-art results, maintaining exceptional subject fidelity and instruction following even over 10+ interaction rounds.

</details>


### [47] [Battery detection of XRay images using transfer learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11779)
*Nermeen Abou Baker,David Rohrschneider,Uwe Handmann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This study uses transfer learning to detect and classify Lithium-Ion Batteries (LIB) in images, achieving 94% precision with 22 ms inference time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The increasing need for detecting and sorting batteries in various applications.

Method: Transfer learning using pre-trained YOLOv5m weights to detect and classify LIB, specifically prismatic, pouch, and cylindrical types.

Result: 94% precision in battery detection with 22 ms inference time, outperforming pre-trained YOLOv5m weights by 5%.

Conclusion: The study demonstrates the potential of transfer learning in accurately detecting and classifying LIB, with promising results for practical applications.

Abstract: The need for detecting and sorting batteries is drastically increasing for many applications. This study proves the potential of transfer learning in predicting whether the image contains a battery or not, the location and identifying three types of batteries, namely: prismatic, pouch, and cylindrical Lithium-Ion Batteries (LIB). Particularly, it focuses on the transfer learning method in two applications: Training a large-scale dataset to detect electronic devices using a pre-trained YOLOv5m, then using these latter trained weights to detect and classify the batteries. The precision of battery detection achieves 94%, which outperforms the pretrained YOLOv5m weights with 5%, in 22 ms inference time.

</details>


### [48] [Seeing What Matters: Perceptual Wrapper with Common Randomness for 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11782)
*He-Bi Yang,Jing-Zhong Chen,Yen-Kuan Ho,Sang NguyenQuang,Fan-Yi Hsu,Yun-Yu Lee,Jui-Chiu Chiang,Wen-Hsiao Peng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A 2D perceptual wrapper is proposed to enhance 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) rendered outputs with improved high-frequency textures, especially under memory constraints and rate-distortion optimization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 3DGS struggles with synthesizing high-frequency textures, particularly in memory-constrained and rate-distortion-optimized pipelines.

Method: A lightweight synthesis network conditioned on pseudo-random Gaussian noise, supervised by Wasserstein Distortion, to match local feature statistics and mitigate blurriness.

Result: The proposed method significantly improves over existing baselines, yielding superior perceptual quality at reduced file or model sizes across various 3DGS methods.

Conclusion: The proposed 2D perceptual wrapper enhances 3DGS rendered outputs with improved high-frequency textures, achieving superior perceptual quality under various constraints.

Abstract: While 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) achieves impressive real-time rendering, it frequently struggles to synthesize high-frequency textures, a limitation heavily exacerbated in memory-constrained and rate-distortion-optimized (RDO) pipelines. To address this, we propose a versatile 2D perceptual wrapper that enhances the rendered outputs of existing 3DGS representations in a content- and view-dependent manner. Our method leverages a lightweight synthesis network conditioned on pseudo-random Gaussian noise to synthesize perceptually plausible textures. Supervised by Wasserstein Distortion, the network learns to match local feature statistics rather than strictly enforcing pixel-wise reconstruction fidelity, effectively mitigating the blurriness inherent in standard frameworks. We demonstrate the broad applicability of our plug-and-play approach across vanilla, memory-constrained, and RDO 3DGS methods. Comprehensive subjective and objective experiments confirm that our method significantly improves over existing baselines, yielding superior perceptual quality at sharply reduced file or model sizes.

</details>


### [49] [A Comprehensive Ecosystem for Open-Domain Customized Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11783)
*Jingxu Zhang,Yuqian Hong,Daneul Kim,Kai Qiu,Qi Dai,Jianmin Bao,Yifan Yang,Xiaoyan Sun,Chong Luo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new dataset and model for customized video generation, achieving state-of-the-art results and providing an open-source ecosystem for further research.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The lack of large-scale, annotated datasets for diverse identity-specific attributes limits open-domain customized video generation.

Method: The authors propose CustoMDiT, a parameter-efficient framework that adapts a pretrained multimodal Diffusion Transformer, and introduce PexelsCustom-1M, a million-scale dataset for identity-preserving video generation.

Result: The method surpasses prior state-of-the-art results, and extensive experiments confirm the advantages of both the dataset and model.

Conclusion: The paper presents a significant contribution to customized video generation, providing a large-scale dataset, a novel model, and an open-source ecosystem for future research.

Abstract: Recent progress in video generation has shown impressive visual synthesis capabilities. However, open-domain customized video generation remains limited by the lack of large-scale, annotated datasets capturing diverse identity-specific attributes. To address this, we introduce PexelsCustom-1M, the first publicly available million-scale dataset for identity-preserving video generation, containing one million curated <identity, text, video> triplets across 8,000+ categories. Leveraging this, we propose CustoMDiT, a parameter-efficient framework that adapts a pretrained multimodal Diffusion Transformer into a customized video generator with only 8% additional learnable parameters. Our method surpasses prior state-of-the-art. However, benchmarks such as DreamBooth cover only 100 classes, which is insufficient for real-world applications. To overcome this, we construct OpenCustom, a new benchmark with 1,000+ categories, created via cross-dataset knowledge fusion from ImageNet and MS-COCO. Extensive experiments confirm the advantages of both our dataset and model. We will open-source the entire ecosystem--including dataset, pipeline, benchmark, and implementations--to support further research.

</details>


### [50] [MultiToP: Learning to Patch Visual Tokens to Mitigate Hallucinations in Video Large Multimodal Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11792)
*Yuansheng Gao,Wenbin Xing,Jiahao Yuan,Kaiwen Zhou,Han Bao,Zonghui Wang,Wenzhi Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new framework, MultiToP, is proposed to mitigate hallucinations in video large multimodal models by refining unreliable visual tokens before language generation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Video large multimodal models are prone to hallucinations, where generated responses are not faithfully supported by the input video.

Method: MultiToP introduces a lightweight Visual Token Patcher and information-guided rank calibration to selectively substitute unreliable visual tokens.

Result: MultiToP reduces hallucinations on Vript-HAL and improves F1 scores by 50.60% and yields an 18.58% relative accuracy gain on ActivityNet-QA.

Conclusion: MultiToP effectively mitigates hallucinations in video large multimodal models without modifying the original model.

Abstract: Video Large Multimodal Models have achieved remarkable progress in video understanding, yet they remain prone to hallucinations, where generated responses are not faithfully supported by the input video. In this paper, we propose MultiToP, a multimodal-context-aware visual token patching framework that mitigates hallucinations by refining unreliable visual tokens before language generation. MultiToP introduces a lightweight Visual Token Patcher to predict token-level replacement distributions and selectively substitute unreliable visual tokens with a dynamic global patch token. To train the patcher effectively, we further propose information-guided rank calibration, which uses answer-conditioned frame-level information cues derived from the backbone to guide token replacement. Combined with ground-truth answer supervision and sparsity regularization, MultiToP enables localized visual evidence refinement without modifying the original model. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MultiToP effectively reduces hallucinations on Vript-HAL with negligible inference overhead, improving the F1 scores of Qwen3-VL-4B-Instruct by 50.60% over the vanilla model. Meanwhile, MultiToP preserves general video understanding ability, yielding an 18.58% relative accuracy gain on ActivityNet-QA for Video-LLaVA-7B.

</details>


### [51] [TextHOI-3D: Text-to-3D Hand-Object Interaction via Discrete Multi-View Generation and Joint Mesh Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11805)
*Zixiong Hao,Zhencun Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TextHOI-3D is a framework for generating 3D hand-object meshes from text, achieving better results by using multi-view observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current methods for text-conditioned 3D generation struggle with producing hand-object meshes that preserve language semantics, cross-view consistency, object geometry, articulated hand shape, and physically plausible contact.

Method: TextHOI-3D uses a staged framework with generated multi-view observations, learning a compact VQ token space, predicting multi-view visual tokens from text, and recovering a unified hand-object mesh through prior initialization, multi-view joint optimization, and anti-penetration refinement.

Result: The multi-view setting reduces object CD from 17.26 mm to 4.92 mm and penetration volume from 5.3721 cm^3 to 0.2193 cm^3, while improving hand errors and surface F-scores.

Conclusion: The results support multi-view visual tokens as an effective intermediate representation for text-driven 3D hand-object mesh creation.

Abstract: Text-conditioned 3D generation has progressed rapidly for images and isolated objects, but producing a hand-object mesh remains challenging: the output must preserve language semantics, cross-view consistency, object geometry, articulated hand shape, and physically plausible contact. We present TextHOI-3D, a staged framework that uses generated multi-view observations as an explicit interface between text-conditioned visual generation and geometry-aware hand-object recovery. TextHOI-3D learns a compact VQ token space for fixed-camera hand-object observations, predicts multi-view visual tokens from text with a CLIP-conditioned visual autoregressive model, and recovers a unified hand-object mesh through prior initialization, multi-view joint optimization, and anti-penetration refinement. The design separates semantic generation from geometric recovery while keeping both stages connected by a discrete multi-view representation. On HO3D-derived evaluations, the multi-view setting reduces object CD from 17.26 mm to 4.92 mm and penetration volume from 5.3721 cm^3 to 0.2193 cm^3 compared with a single-view counterpart, while improving hand errors and surface F-scores. These results support multi-view visual tokens as an effective intermediate representation for text-driven 3D hand-object mesh creation.

</details>


### [52] [LASA: A Weak Supervision Method for Open-Vocabulary Scene Sketch Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11837)
*Liwen Yi,Xianlin Zhang,Yue Zhang,Yue Ming,Xueming Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a structure-aware framework called LASA for open-vocabulary scene sketch semantic segmentation, which aggregates multi-layer attention to improve segmentation accuracy and spatial coherence under weak supervision.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The task of open-vocabulary scene sketch semantic segmentation is challenging due to the lack of texture and color cues in sketches, making semantic understanding heavily dependent on stroke layout and spatial configuration.

Method: The proposed framework, LASA, leverages layer-wise accumulated structural attention to aggregate multi-layer attention and guide hierarchical semantic alignment under weak supervision.

Result: The experiments show that LASA improves mIoU by +3.43, +8.01, and +15.74 over the prior weakly supervised baselines on FS-COCO, SFSD, and FrISS datasets, respectively.

Conclusion: The proposed LASA framework demonstrates consistent gains in both segmentation accuracy and spatial coherence for open-vocabulary scene sketch semantic segmentation under weak supervision.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary scene sketch semantic segmentation aims to assign dense semantic labels to sparse line drawings based on flexible category vocabularies specified at inference time, without relying on pixel-level annotations during training. Unlike natural images, sketches lack texture and color cues, making semantic understanding heavily dependent on stroke layout and spatial configuration, a challenge that renders single-layer vision-language features inherently unstable. Our key observation is that attention maps from different Vision Transformer layers encode complementary spatial cues: shallow layers capture global structural layouts, while deeper layers focus on local stroke intersections and object parts. This suggests that cross-layer aggregation provides a more robust structural prior than any individual layer alone. Leveraging this insight, we propose a structure-aware framework built upon \textbf{L}ayer-wise \textbf{A}ccumulated \textbf{S}tructural \textbf{A}ttention (\textbf{LASA}), which aggregates multi-layer attention to guide hierarchical semantic alignment under weak supervision and refine predictions during inference. Experiments on FS-COCO, SFSD, and FrISS show that LASA improves mIoU by $+3.43$, $+8.01$, and $+15.74$ over the prior weakly supervised baselines, demonstrating consistent gains in both segmentation accuracy and spatial coherence. Our source code will be made publicly available.

</details>


### [53] [Plan-and-Verify Video Reward Reasoning with Spatio-Temporal Scene Graph Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11838)
*Hyomin Kim,Junghye Kim,Joanie Hayoun Chung,Yoonjin Oh,Kyungjae Lee,Sungbin Lim,Sungwoong Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes SG-PVR, a video reward model that improves fine-grained semantic alignment in text-to-video generation by using plan-and-verify reasoning grounded in spatio-temporal scene graphs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing reward models for text-to-video generation often fail at fine-grained semantic alignment due to structural weaknesses in their reasoning processes.

Method: The proposed SG-PVR model uses plan-and-verify reasoning grounded in spatio-temporal scene graphs to verify every condition described in the prompt and provide explicit visual evidence for judgments.

Result: SG-PVR achieves strong performance on semantic alignment, including fine-grained temporal semantics, and enhances compositional alignment in text-to-video generation as a test-time reranker.

Conclusion: The proposed SG-PVR model effectively addresses the limitations of existing reward models and improves fine-grained semantic alignment in text-to-video generation.

Abstract: Reward models for text-to-video (T2V) generation guide post-training but often fail at fine-grained semantic alignment. We trace this to two structural weaknesses in existing reasoning-based reward models: they do not systematically verify every condition described in the prompt, and the visual evidence supporting each judgment remains implicit in their free-form reasoning. We propose SG-PVR, a video reward model that addresses these limitations through plan-and-verify reasoning grounded in spatio-temporal scene graphs. The verification plan decomposes the prompt into atomic claims, ensuring every requirement is checked. The spatio-temporal scene graph, encoding entities, attributes, and temporally-grounded relations, is extracted from the video and maintained as a persistent structured visual reference throughout reasoning. Each claim is verified against both the video and the scene graph, anchoring judgments in explicit visual evidence. SG-PVR achieves strong performance on semantic alignment, including fine-grained temporal semantics. As a test-time reranker, it further enhances compositional alignment in T2V generation.

</details>


### [54] [Scene-Adaptive Nonlinear Tone Curves for Pseudo Ground-Truth Generation in Low-Light 3D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11841)
*Mingzhe Lyu,Jinqiang Cui,Hong Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel framework for generating pseudo ground-truth images in low-light novel view synthesis using nonlinear tone mappings, leading to improved reconstruction quality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing pseudo-GT methods for 3D reconstruction in low-light conditions are limited by their use of uniform linear gain, which can clip bright regions and provide insufficient enhancement in dark regions.

Method: A scene-adaptive nonlinear tone-curve framework that introduces percentile-based normalization, scene-adaptive offset, and two complementary curves: Adaptive SoftExp (ASE) and Adaptive Poly3 (AP3).

Result: The proposed framework consistently outperforms the linear baseline with PSNR improvements up to +4.34 dB on LOM and +3.25 dB on RealX3D.

Conclusion: The proposed nonlinear tone-curve framework leads to improved reconstruction quality in low-light novel view synthesis, and the improvement is curve-agnostic.

Abstract: Low-light novel view synthesis is challenging because dark multi-view images contain noise, weak structural detail, and compressed dynamic range. Recent 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods address these challenges by generating pseudo ground-truth (pseudo-GT) images as supervision targets when paired normal-light references are unavailable. Existing pseudo-GT methods apply a uniform linear gain to all pixels, which clips bright regions while providing insufficient enhancement in dark regions, limiting reconstruction quality. We observe that nonlinear tone mappings, long established in 2D low-light enhancement, have not been explored for pseudo-GT generation in 3D reconstruction. Accordingly, we propose a scene-adaptive nonlinear tone-curve framework that replaces linear pseudo-GT with nonlinear alternatives. The framework introduces percentile-based normalisation for scene-agnostic curve application, a scene-adaptive offset for automatic black-level adjustment, and two complementary curves: Adaptive SoftExp (ASE), a bounded exponential curve, and Adaptive Poly3 (AP3), a data-driven cubic polynomial. The module changes only the pseudo-GT computation and leaves the 3DGS backbone unchanged. Experiments on three benchmarks covering 21 scenes show that both curves consistently outperform the linear baseline with PSNR improvements up to +4.34 dB on LOM and +3.25 dB on RealX3D. Both curves achieve similar performance despite their different mathematical forms, suggesting the improvement is curve-agnostic. Code is available at https://github.com/lvmingzhe/adaptiveToneCurve

</details>


### [55] [SheafStain: Sheaf-Theoretic Schrödinger Bridge for Spatially and Biologically Coherent Virtual Staining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11846)
*Hyeongyeol Lim,Hongjun Yoon,Eunjin Jang,Daeky Jeong,Won June Cho,Hwamin Lee*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The authors propose SheafStain, a new approach for virtual staining in cancer diagnostics, which uses a sheaf-theoretic framework to maintain spatial continuity and biological consistency in gigapixel whole slide images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current virtual staining approaches have limitations in maintaining spatial continuity and consistency in gigapixel whole slide images, leading to artifacts and mismatches with ground-truth images.

Method: The authors propose SheafStain, which reinterprets pathology Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) features as sheaf-like sections, integrating class and patch tokens into a Schrödinger Bridge framework.

Result: SheafStain demonstrates promising results against six prior methods in virtual staining for cancer diagnostics, mitigating patch-boundary stitching artifacts and maintaining spatial continuity and biological consistency.

Conclusion: The proposed SheafStain approach offers a novel solution for virtual staining in cancer diagnostics, addressing the limitations of current approaches and demonstrating improved results in maintaining spatial continuity and biological consistency.

Abstract: Current virtual staining approaches offer the potential for time- and cost-efficient biomarker quantification in cancer diagnostics and prognostics. However, patch-wise inference for gigapixel whole slide images (WSIs) fails to maintain spatial continuity, yielding artifacts that cause catastrophic mismatches with ground-truth images. Although pathology Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) offer rich representations, their self-attention causes varying global contexts to produce inconsistent embeddings for the same physical region. We formalize and validate this ``context contamination'' as a sheaf-theoretic problem where these embeddings form a presheaf that violates the gluing axiom. To address this, we propose SheafStain, a new approach that reinterprets VFM features as sheaf-like sections for spatially and biologically coherent virtual staining. Specifically, SheafStain integrates class and patch tokens into a Schrödinger Bridge framework as sheaf-like sections. While the class token anchors biological consistency, patch tokens form a per-position spatial map. A backbone co-pretrained on Hematoxylin \& Eosin (H\&E) and Immunohistochemistry (IHC) yields non-degenerate cross-stain stalks, so a single VFM feature space supervises both input conditioning and output stain alignment. Departing from prior work that evaluates on isolated $256 \times 256$ patches and either random-crops or resizes the $1024 \times 1024$ ground truth, we translate at $256 \times 256$ and evaluate on the stitched $1024 \times 1024$ outputs across HER2, ER, PR, and Ki-67. SheafStain demonstrates promising results against six prior methods while mitigating patch-boundary stitching artifacts. Code will soon be released.

</details>


### [56] [Task-Aware Structured Memory for Dynamic Multi-modal In-Context Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11853)
*Zhirui Chen,Ziwei Chen,Ling Shao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TASM is a new framework for efficient and adaptable memory construction in multi-modal large language models, enabling task-aware compression and dynamic memory retrieval.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The scalability of multi-modal large language models is limited by finite context windows and the growing cost of key-value caches in long multi-modal sequences.

Method: TASM employs task-vector guided compression and semantics-aware token merging to construct a structured memory hierarchy, comprising a compact Core Memory and a Latent Bank.

Result: Evaluations confirm TASM maintains high performance under heavy compression, effectively balancing efficiency with adaptability.

Conclusion: TASM addresses the limitations of existing memory compression approaches, providing a training-free framework for task-aware, structure-preserving, and dynamically accessible memory construction.

Abstract: Multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) depend on in-context learning (ICL) for rapid task adaptation, but their scalability is severely limited by finite context windows and the growing cost of key-value (KV) caches in long multi-modal sequences. Existing memory compression approaches typically rely on rigid token removal or sample-dependent importance estimation, which introduces bias, disrupts semantic structure, particularly for visual representations, and yields static memories that cannot adapt to new queries. We introduce TASM (Task-Aware Structured Memory), a training-free framework that addresses these limitations through task-aware, structure-preserving, and dynamically accessible memory construction. TASM employs task-vector guided compression to replace sample-specific signals with a task-level direction that captures shared relevance across demonstrations. To preserve the underlying manifold, it applies semantics-aware token merging via bipartite graph matching, aggregating tokens without destructive pruning. Finally, TASM structures memory into a hierarchy comprising a compact Core Memory and a Latent Bank, facilitating query-adaptive dynamic retrieval. Evaluations confirm TASM maintains high performance under heavy compression, effectively balancing efficiency with adaptability.

</details>


### [57] [SG2Loc: Sequential Visual Localization on 3D Scene Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11880)
*Nicole Damblon,Olga Vysotska,Federico Tombari,Marc Pollefeys,Daniel Barath*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A novel, lightweight approach to sequential visual localization in complex indoor environments using 3D scene graphs, reducing storage requirements while maintaining performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional visual localization methods require extensive image databases or point clouds, leading to significant overhead. There is a need for a more efficient approach.

Method: The method uses a compact 3D scene graph to represent the environment, where nodes are objects with coarse meshes and edges encode spatial relationships. It then performs localization within a particle filter framework, using semantic features and object identities.

Result: The approach significantly reduces storage requirements while maintaining performance on real-world datasets.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides a lightweight and efficient solution for sequential visual localization in complex indoor environments, making it suitable for robotics and AR applications.

Abstract: Visual localization in complex indoor environments remains a critical challenge for robotics and AR applications. Sequential localization, where pose estimates are refined over time, is important for autonomous agents. However, traditional methods often require storing extensive image databases or point clouds, leading to significant overhead. This paper introduces a novel, lightweight approach to sequential visual localization using 3D scene graphs. Our method represents the environment with a compact scene graph, where nodes represent objects (with coarse meshes) and edges encode spatial relationships. For each image in the localization phase, we extract per-patch semantic features, predicting object identities. Localization is performed within a particle filter framework. Each particle, representing a camera pose, projects the coarse object meshes from the scene graph into the image, assigning object identities to patches based on visibility. The similarity of the per-patch features, in the input image, and object features from the scene graph determines the weight of a particle. Subsequent images are incorporated sequentially, refining the pose estimate. By leveraging a compact scene graph and efficient semantic matching, our method significantly reduces storage while maintaining performance on real-world datasets. The code will be available at https://github.com/DmblnNicole/sg2loc.

</details>


### [58] [Image Quality Assessment of Identity Cards Using Measures from Open Face Image Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11884)
*Gregor Grote,Juan E. Tapia,Christian Rathgeb*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper evaluates using Open Face Image Quality (OFIQ) measures to assess ID card image quality in remote verification systems, finding that some OFIQ measures can improve presentation attack detection (PAD) performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The challenge of assessing image quality in ID cards in remote verification systems, where poor image quality can affect the performance of presentation attack detection (PAD) algorithms.

Method: Applying capture-related quality measures from the OFIQ standard to ID card images, with a preprocessing pipeline including corner detection, perspective normalization, and foreground masking.

Result: The results suggest that quality assessment based on some OFIQ measures can significantly improve PAD performance across four diverse ID card datasets.

Conclusion: The study concludes that using OFIQ measures can be effective in improving PAD performance in remote ID card verification systems.

Abstract: This paper addresses the challenge of assessing image quality in ID cards in remote verification systems by applying capture-related quality measures from the Open Face Image Quality (OFIQ) standard to ID card images. Our preprocessing pipeline includes corner detection, perspective normalization, and comprehensive foreground masking to ensure accurate and unbiased quality measure computation. We evaluate the effectiveness of these measures by analyzing their correlation with the performance of three presentation attack detection (PAD) algorithms across four diverse ID card datasets, where two datasets contain bona fide, i.e. pristine, images and two contain printed mock ID cards. Our results suggest that quality assessment based on some OFIQ measures can significantly improve PAD performance.

</details>


### [59] [Task-Aligned Stability Analysis of Vision-Language Models for Autonomous Driving Hazard Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11889)
*Everett Richards*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The study examines the relationship between embedding stability and task-aligned hazard scores in vision-language models for autonomous driving, finding a corruption-dependent relationship and highlighting the need for task-aligned stability measures in robustness benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate whether corruption-induced embedding drift predicts changes in task-aligned hazard scores derived from CLIP image-text similarities in vision-language models for autonomous driving.

Method: The study uses controlled corruptions on BDD100K road scenes to compare embedding drift against margin drift, defined as the change in hazard score under perturbation.

Result: The relationship between embedding drift and decision drift is highly corruption-dependent, with some corruption families exhibiting strong coupling and others inducing hazardous decision instability despite modest embedding change.

Conclusion: Robustness benchmarks should include task-aligned stability measures in addition to embedding-level perturbation statistics to account for asymmetric failure modes and not just overall instability rates.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) are increasingly used for scene understanding in autonomous driving, but robustness analysis often relies on task-agnostic embedding stability alone. We study whether corruption-induced embedding drift predicts changes in a task-aligned hazard score derived from CLIP image-text similarities. Using controlled corruptions on BDD100K road scenes, we compare embedding drift against margin drift, defined as the change in hazard score under perturbation. The relationship is highly corruption-dependent: some families exhibit strong coupling between representation drift and decision drift, while others induce hazardous decision instability despite relatively modest embedding change. Furthermore, corruption families differ in failure direction: most suppress hazard detections via false negatives, while occlusion instead triggers false alarms, suggesting that benchmark design should account for asymmetric failure modes, not just overall instability rates. These results suggest that robustness benchmarks should include task-aligned stability measures in addition to embedding-level perturbation statistics.

</details>


### [60] [Wild3R: Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting from Unconstrained Sparse Photo Collection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11894)
*Yuto Furutani,Takashi Otonari,Kaede Shiohara,Toshihiko Yamasaki*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A feed-forward approach for 3D scene representation from sparse photo collections, leveraging a large dataset to achieve robust results under diverse lighting and transient objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing feed-forward approaches for 3D Gaussian Splatting struggle with real-world photo collections due to diverse lighting conditions and transient objects.

Method: The Wild3R approach uses a large dataset, WildCity, to train a model that learns appearance consistency across viewpoints and removes transient content.

Result: The method outperforms existing feed-forward approaches and achieves results competitive with per-scene optimization-based methods.

Conclusion: The proposed Wild3R approach enables robust 3D scene representation from unconstrained sparse photo collections.

Abstract: Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) removes the need for time-consuming per-scene optimization required by traditional 3DGS. However, existing feed-forward approaches struggle with real-world photo collections that include diverse lighting conditions and transient objects. In this paper, we present Wild3R, a feed-forward approach for unconstrained sparse photo collections. The main bottleneck is the lack of training data that provides multiple viewpoints, a variety of illuminations, and transient variations necessary for learning robust scene representations. To address this, we introduce the WildCity dataset, which comprises 200 scenes, 170 lighting conditions, and transient objects, resulting in 337,500 images in total. By leveraging the dataset, our model learns appearance consistency across viewpoints conditioned on reference views, while removing transient content. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method outperforms existing feed-forward approaches and achieves results competitive with prior per-scene optimization-based methods.

</details>


### [61] [From Content to Knowledge: Lightning Fast Long-Video Understanding with Neural Knowledge Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11913)
*Yuchen Guan,Xiao Li,Zongyu Guo,Xiaoyi Zhang,Xiulian Peng,Chun Yuan,Yan Lu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Proposed a new paradigm for long video understanding by representing video content as Neural Knowledge Representation (NKR) and optimizing it via Agentic Knowledge Distillation (AKD) for efficient query-based understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods for long video understanding are inefficient due to the need for re-encoding or reloading the video for each query, motivating the need for a more efficient approach.

Method: The proposed method uses Neural Knowledge Representation (NKR) to represent video content and optimizes it via Agentic Knowledge Distillation (AKD) for efficient query-based understanding.

Result: The proposed method achieved performance comparable to state-of-the-art approaches while reducing end-to-end latency by over two orders of magnitude.

Conclusion: The proposed paradigm offers a more efficient approach for interactive long-video understanding by decoupling video length from inference cost.

Abstract: We propose a new paradigm for long video understanding by treating a long video as a Neural Knowledge Representation (NKR). NKR represents video contents neither as a stream of tokens nor pre-organized databases, but as an individual small portion of network weights attached to the VLM backbone. The NKR weights are optimized to encapsulate the video's semantic content via a novel Agentic Knowledge Distillation (AKD) process, where an agent automatically synthesizes dense descriptions and question-answer pairs to distill the video's knowledge into the NKR. While AKD serves as a comprehensive, one-time encoding phase, the resulting NKR transforms the video into a portable, reusable asset. At inference, the lightweight NKR is mounted onto a frozen Vision-Language Model (VLM), enabling direct, query-based understanding without reloading or re-encoding the original video. This approach decouples video length from inference cost, offering high amortized efficiency for multi-turn video understanding. Experiments on the LVBench benchmark show our method achieves performance comparable to state-of-the-art approaches while reducing end-to-end latency by over two orders of magnitude, opening new possibilities for interactive long-video understanding.

</details>


### [62] [Corpus Augmentation for Sign Language Translation via LLM-Guided Video Stitching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11925)
*Zsolt Robotka,Ádám Rák,Jalal Al-Afandi,András Horváth,György Cserey*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a corpus augmentation approach for sign language translation (SLT) that generates synthetic RGB video-text pairs from existing gloss-annotated training corpora and a large language model (LLM), improving performance without requiring additional human annotation or external resources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The scarcity of high-quality parallel sign video-text pairs for fine-tuning limits the generalisation of SLT models on long-tail vocabulary and unseen constructions.

Method: The approach involves extracting per-gloss clips from training videos, generating novel gloss-sentence pairs using a corpus-anchored LLM, and assembling synthetic sequences through random sentence sampling and clip assignment.

Result: The proposed augmentation achieves a +2.92 BLEU-4 improvement over the baseline, outperforming recent gloss-free methods and demonstrating the effectiveness of synthetic data in SLT.
